## Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.16

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.16 — Roman transliteration

saṅkarṣaṇas tāḥ kṛṣṇasya
sandeśair hṛdayaṃ-gamaiḥ
sāntvayām āsa bhagavān
nānānunaya-kovidaḥ

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: scripture_root_text, supreme_personality_of_godhead, stance_descriptive, exact_root_text; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.16; retrieval-intents: exact_root_text.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.16 — Translation

The Supreme Lord Balarāma, the attractor of all, being expert at various kinds of conciliation, consoled the gopīs by relaying to them the confidential messages Lord Kṛṣṇa had sent with Him. These messages deeply touched the gopīs’ hearts.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.16; persons: Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.16 — Purport

Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī quotes the following verse from Śrī Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5.24.20), which describes the messages Lord Balarāma brought from Kṛṣṇa for the gopīs:

sandeśaiḥ sāma-madhuraiḥ

prema-garbhair agarvitaiḥ

rāmeṇāśvāsitā gopyaḥ

kṛṣṇasyāti-manoharaiḥ

“Lord Balarāma consoled the gopīs by giving them Lord Kṛṣṇa’s most charming messages, which expressed sweet conciliation, which were inspired by His pure love for them, and which were without a tinge of pride.” Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī also comments that the use of the name Saṅkarṣaṇa here implies that Balarāma attracted Lord Kṛṣṇa to appear in His mind and in this way showed Śrī Kṛṣṇa to the gopīs. Thus Balarāma consoled Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s beloved girlfriends.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī comments that Lord Kṛṣṇa sent various messages. Some instructed the gopīs in transcendental knowledge, others were conciliatory, and still others revealed the Lord’s power. Besides its given meaning, the word hṛdayaṃ-gamaiḥ also indicates that these messages were confidential.

## Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.17

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.17 — Roman transliteration

dvau māsau tatra cāvātsīn

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: scripture_root_text, supreme_personality_of_godhead, stance_descriptive, exact_root_text; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.17; retrieval-intents: exact_root_text.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.17 — Translation

Lord Balarāma, the Personality of Godhead, resided there for the two months of Madhu and Mādhava, and during the nights He gave His cowherd girlfriends conjugal pleasure.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.17; persons: Balarāma; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.17 — Purport

Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī states that the gopīs who enjoyed conjugal affairs with Śrī Balarāma during His visit to Gokula had not taken part in Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s rāsa dance, being too young at the time. Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī confirms this statement by quoting a phrase from the Bhāgavatam (10.15.8)—gopyo ’ntareṇa bhujayoḥ—which indicates that there are particular gopīs who act as Lord Balarāma’s girlfriends. Furthermore, Jīva Gosvāmī states that during the Holī festivities celebrated when Kṛṣṇa killed Śaṅkhacūḍa, the gopīs Lord Balarāma enjoyed with were different from the ones Lord Kṛṣṇa enjoyed with. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī agrees with this explanation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.19 — Translation

Sent by the demigod Varuṇa, the divine Vāruṇī liquor flowed from a tree hollow and made the entire forest even more fragrant with its sweet aroma.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.19; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.19 — Purport

Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī explains that Vāruṇī is a liquor distilled from honey. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī adds that the goddess Vāruṇī, the daughter of Varuṇa, is the presiding deity of that particular divine liquor. The ācārya also quotes the following statement from Śrī Hari-vaṃśa: samīpaṃ preṣitā pitrā varuṇena tavānagha. Here the goddess Vāruṇī says to Lord Balarāma: “My father, Varuṇa, has sent me to You, O sinless one.”

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.24–25 — Translation

This angered Balarāma, and He began dragging the river with the tip of His plow.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: scripture_root_text, stance_descriptive, exact_root_text; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.27; retrieval-intents: exact_root_text.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.27 — Translation

[Śukadeva Gosvāmī continued:] Thus scolded by the Lord, O King, the frightened river-goddess Yamunā came and fell at the feet of Śrī Balarāma, the beloved descendant of Yadu. Trembling, she spoke to Him the following words.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, pedagogy_logical_argument, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.27; persons: Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Balarāma; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.27 — Purport

According to Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, the goddess who appeared before Lord Balarāma is an expansion of Śrīmatī Kālindī, one of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s queens in Dvārakā. Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī calls her a “shadow” of Kālindī, and Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī confirms that she is an expansion of Kālindī, not Kālindī herself. Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī also gives evidence from Śrī Hari-vaṃśa—in the statement pratyuvācārṇava-vadhūm—that Goddess Yamunā is the wife of the ocean. The Hari-vaṃśa therefore also refers to her as sāgarāṅganā.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.31 — Translation

The Lord played in the water to His full satisfaction, and when He came out Goddess Kānti presented Him with blue garments, precious ornaments and a brilliant necklace.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.31; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.31 — Purport

Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī quotes from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa to show that the Goddess Kānti mentioned here is actually Lakṣmī, the goddess of fortune:

varuṇa-prahitā cāsmai

mālām amlāna-paṅkajām

samudrābhe tathā vastre

nīle lakṣmīr ayacchata

“Sent by Varuna, Goddess Lakṣmī then presented Him with a garland of unfading lotuses and a pair of garments colored blue like the ocean.”

The great Bhāgavatam commentator Śrīla Śrīdhara Svāmī also quotes the following statement from Śrī Hari-vaṃśa, spoken by Goddess Lakṣmī to Lord Balarāma:

jātarūpa-mayaṃ caikaṃ

kuṇḍalaṃ vajra-bhūṣaṇam

ādi-padmaṃ ca padmākhyaṃ

divyaṃ śravaṇa-bhūṣaṇam

devemāṃ pratigṛhṇīṣva

paurāṇīṃ bhūṣaṇa-kriyām

“O Lord, please accept as divine ornaments for Your ears this single gold earring studded with diamonds and this primeval lotus called Padma. Kindly accept them, for this act of adornment is traditional.”

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī further points out that Goddess Lakṣmī is the consort of the Lord’s plenary expansion Saṅkarṣaṇa who belongs to the second vyūha.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.32 — Translation

Lord Balarāma dressed Himself in the blue garments and put on the gold necklace. Anointed with fragrances and beautifully adorned, He appeared as resplendent as Indra’s royal elephant.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.32 — Purport

Anointed with sandalwood paste and other pure, fragrant substances, Balarāma resembled Airāvata, the great elephant of Lord Indra.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: scripture_root_text, stance_descriptive, exact_root_text; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.34; retrieval-intents: exact_root_text.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.34 — Translation

Thus for Lord Balarāma all the nights passed like a single night as He enjoyed in Vraja, His mind enchanted by the exquisite charm and beauty of Vraja’s young ladies.

> Search metadata — semantic-tags: verse_translation, prabhupada_translation, pedagogy_analogy, pedagogy_logical_argument, stance_descriptive, exact_translation, short_quotation; authority: disciple completion / retelling; canonical scripture; primary-reference-aliases: SB 10.65.34; persons: Balarāma; retrieval-intents: exact_translation, short_quotation.

### Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.65.34 — Purport

Lord Balarāma was enchanted by the charming pastimes of the beautiful young ladies of Vraja. Thus each night was a completely new experience, and all the nights passed as if they were a single night.

Thus end the purports of the humble servants of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda to the Tenth Canto, Sixty-fifth Chapter, of the Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, entitled “Lord Balarāma Visits Vṛndāvana.”
